<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that critical FK–Ising interfaces with sufficiently weak, symmetric, independent two-valued bond disorder converge to chordal SLE<sub>16/3</sub>. The disorder strength is fixed as the mesh tends to zero, and convergence of the conditional curve laws holds in probability over the environment.

- **claim-main-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exists $\varepsilon_0>0$ such that for every fixed $0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_0$, every bounded marked Jordan domain $(D;a,b)$, and every deterministic filled square-lattice approximation satisfying <a href="#eq:jordan-approximation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:jordan-approximation">[eq:jordan-approximation]</a>, $$d_{\mathrm{BL}}\bigl(\mu_{n,\xi}, \operatorname{Law}(\mathrm{SLE}_{16/3}(D;a,b))\bigr) \xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{\mathbb P_{\mathrm{env}}}0.$$ The constant $\varepsilon_0$ is independent of the domain and its approximation.
  - Explanation: Main result: for weak symmetric two-valued bond disorder, the quenched law of the FK–Ising exploration interface converges in probability over the environment to the chordal SLE law, with a disorder threshold independent of the domain and its approximation.
- **claim-critical-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-critical-input)): For sufficiently small $\varepsilon>0$, the plus-magnetization threshold for $J_e=1+\varepsilon\xi_e$ is the unique positive solution of $$\bigl(e^{2\beta_c(\varepsilon)(1+\varepsilon)}-1\bigr) \bigl(e^{2\beta_c(\varepsilon)(1-\varepsilon)}-1\bigr)=2. \label{eq:critical-self-duality}$$ The plus magnetization vanishes at this temperature. If $v_\pm=e^{2\beta_c(\varepsilon)(1\pm\varepsilon)}-1$ and $\theta=\frac12\log(v_+/v_-)$, then $$v_e=\sqrt2\,e^{\theta\xi_e},\qquad \theta\longrightarrow0 \quad\text{as }\varepsilon\downarrow0.$$ The scale trajectory with vanishing perturbations constructed in [[Q](#ref-Q)] occurs at $h=0$ in <a href="#eq:critical-coordinate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:critical-coordinate">[eq:critical-coordinate]</a>.
  - Explanation: Imported from the companion work: the physical critical inverse temperature is fixed by a self-duality equation, the magnetization vanishes there, and the effective log-odds disorder tends to zero with the bond disorder.
- **claim-barrier-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-barrier-input)): There are fixed $a_0>1$ and $c_0>0$ such that the following holds at $\beta_c(\varepsilon)$. Let $B_r(x)=\{y\in\mathbb Z^2:\left\lVert y-x\right\rVert_\infty\le r\}$, and let $\mathcal A(x,r)=B_{a_0r}(x)\setminus B_r(x)$ be its lattice annulus. With $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{env}}$-probability tending to one as $r\to\infty$, $$\phi_{\mathcal A(x,r),J}^{\mathrm w} (\text{ordinary open radial crossing})\le1-c_0. \label{eq:barrier-input}$$ Here both rims are wired together. The convergence is uniform in $x$, and the event that <a href="#eq:barrier-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:barrier-input">[eq:barrier-input]</a> holds depends only on the bonds of the annulus and its incident lattice layer. The same bound holds for every less wired boundary condition. The assertion also holds for dual crossings with dual odds.
  - Explanation: Imported local barrier: at the critical point a radial open crossing of a lattice annulus with wired rims has probability bounded away from one, with environmental probability tending to one; no rate is given.
- **claim-bulk-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-bulk-input)): There is a fixed support constant $C_{\rm s}$ with the following properties. Choose a sufficiently large odd step factor $L$, a sufficiently small flag threshold parameter $b_0>0$ thereafter, and a sufficiently large fixed $s_0$. For all sufficiently small $\theta>0$, the plane fields <a href="#eq:initial-logs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:initial-logs">[eq:initial-logs]</a> at $h=0$ admit successive transformations to potentials $f_i^{(k)}$ at spacings $s_k=s_0L^k$ such that:* 1. *Each $f_i^{(k)}$ is a bounded real spin-flip-even function, is centered by $\mathbb E_0 f_i^{(k)}=0$, and has field support and environmental dependence in the square of radius $C_{\rm s}s_k$ about $s_ki$. The primitive environmental variables are independent. Collections with disjoint dependence neighborhoods are independent as random functions. The plane field is stationary on its grid and square symmetric in distribution.* 2. *With the fixed moment order $P=40$, $$X_k=\bigl(\mathbb E\left\lVert f_i^{(k)}\right\rVert_\infty^P\bigr)^{1/P} \longrightarrow0. \label{eq:bulk-decay}$$ The numbers $X_k$ are uniformly arbitrarily small when $\theta$ is sufficiently small. The disorder bound and construction parameters are chosen independently of any domain or mesh approximation.* 3. *Every local replacement preserves exterior-integrated weights exactly, up to field-independent centering factors. This holds for arbitrary assignments of the old exterior field whenever the complete local construction is performed. For numerical $K_{\rm s}$ and a constant $C_L$, one scale step satisfies $$\left\lVert f'_j\right\rVert_\infty\le C_L\sum_{i:\,\left\lVert si-Lsj\right\rVert_\infty\le K_{\rm s}Ls} \left\lVert f_i\right\rVert_\infty. \label{eq:map-raw-input}$$ The formulas are local and agree in embedded plane and torus charts.* *Any additional fixed lower bound on $L$, upper bound on $b_0$, or lower bound on $s_0$ can be imposed in this order, followed by decreasing the allowed disorder interval.
  - Explanation: Imported bulk renormalization: successive local scale transformations give bounded centered potentials with local dependence whose moments tend to zero, preserve exterior-integrated weights exactly and satisfy a local one-step norm bound.
- **claim-boundary-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-transfer)): There are constants $d_0>1$ and $C<\infty$ with the following properties for the pure shell kernels just defined.* 1. *At a straight side, if both rays are pinned plus, then for every fixed $d=R/r\ge d_0$, $$\Delta(T)\le C d^{-2}. \label{eq:straight-boundary-transfer}$$ If both rays are free, the same bound holds for $\overline T$.* 2. *At a corner with both rays pinned plus or both rays free, there is a fixed ratio $d_0$ for which the appropriate kernel has projective diameter at most $C$. The same finite bound holds at a straight boundary-condition change, with one ray pinned plus and the other free.* *In each statement the mesh is sufficiently fine relative to $r$. The threshold may depend on the fixed ratio and the finite collection of cap shapes. The constants are uniform under translation, lattice rounding, and bounded lattice-layer displacements of the cuts and dual boundaries.
  - Explanation: Pure Ising shell kernels near a boundary lose dependence on distant spins: the projective diameter decays quadratically in the scale ratio along a straight side and is merely bounded at corners and at changes of boundary condition.
- **claim-boundary-observables** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-boundary-observables)): Let a spin patch of diameter at most $r$ be separated from prescribed remote spins $\zeta$ by pure shells of one of the types in Lemma <a href="#lem:boundary-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:boundary-transfer">6</a>, out to distance $R=dr$. Allow fixed proportional buffers around the patch and the outer cut. Let $H$ be a bounded real function of the patch spins and of any independent auxiliary variables in the patch. In the free/free case assume that the region inside the shell has no pinned spins and that $H$ is invariant under reversal of all its spin arguments. There are constants $C<\infty$ and $\eta>0$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_\zeta\mathbb E_{\mathrm{pure}}[H\mid\zeta] &\le C a(d)\\|H\\|_\infty, \label{eq:boundary-expectation-transfer}\\ \mathop{\mathrm{osc}}_\zeta \log\mathbb E_{\mathrm{pure}}[e^H\mid\zeta] &\le C a(d)\\|H\\|_\infty, \label{eq:boundary-log-transfer} \end{aligned}$$ where $a(d)=d^{-2}$ at a straight homogeneous side and $a(d)=d^{-\eta}$ at a corner or boundary-condition change. Here $\mathop{\mathrm{osc}} f=\sup f-\inf f$. The bounds hold for each fixed sufficiently large $d$ and sufficiently fine mesh, with thresholds chosen simultaneously for any finite collection of ratios.
  - Explanation: Consequence of the kernel bound: conditional expectations and logarithms of exponential moments of a bounded local function oscillate little over remote spins, with a power decay in the scale ratio.
- **claim-polygon-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-polygon-comparison)): There exists $\theta_0>0$ with the following property. Fix $0<\theta<\theta_0$, a collared orthogonal polygon $\mathcal P$, and four distinct boundary marks in straight side interiors. Wire each of two opposite marked boundary arcs separately and leave the intervening arcs free. On the rounded lifted grid assign odds $$v_e=\sqrt2\exp(\theta\xi_e),$$ where the physical-edge signs are independent and symmetric, and repeated lifted edges have the same sign. Let $\phi_{\delta,J}^{\rm sep}$ and $\phi_{\delta,0}^{\rm sep}$ denote respectively this random-cluster law and the pure law on the same graph and with the same boundary partition. If $\mathcal A_\delta$ is the event of an actual open path between the two wired arcs, then $$\label{eq:polygon-comparison} \phi_{\delta,J}^{\rm sep}(\mathcal A_\delta) -\phi_{\delta,0}^{\rm sep}(\mathcal A_\delta) \longrightarrow0 \qquad\text{in environmental probability as }\delta\downarrow0.$$ The disorder threshold is independent of the fixed polygon and marks. The conclusion also holds on the dual square grid.
  - Explanation: On a fixed collared polygon with separately wired opposite arcs, the actual crossing probability under weak disorder differs from the pure one by an amount tending to zero in environmental probability; the threshold does not depend on the polygon.
- **claim-boundary-exactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-exactness)): For the terminal scale chosen above, the local construction is exact for each fixed environment and realization of its priorities and thresholds. Its potentials have support and environmental dependence radius at most $Cs_k$. Outside the boundary cells they are the plane potentials, or zero outside the pad. The two pin assignments admit the same scalar subtractions. More precisely, there is a scalar $a_\delta$, independent of the pin assignment, such that $$\label{eq:polygon-exact-partition} \frac{\mathcal Z_J^\eta}{\mathcal Z_0^\eta} =e^{a_\delta}\mathbb E_0^\eta \exp\!\left(\sum_i f_i^{(N),\eta}\right), \qquad \eta\in\{++, +-\}.$$ The initial boundary norms are uniformly $O_{s_0}(\theta)$.
  - Explanation: The scale construction with the boundary present is exact, has local support, and gives the partition-function ratio as a common scalar times a pure expectation of an exponential of terminal potentials, for both pin assignments.
- **claim-boundary-recursion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-recursion)): The scale-step parameters can be chosen in the order: $L$ sufficiently large, $b_0$ sufficiently small, and $s_0$ sufficiently large, so that for all $k<N$, $$\label{eq:boundary-recurrence} Z_{k+1}\le q_* Z_k+C_LX_k +C_{L,b_0}(Z_k+X_k)^2, \qquad q_*<1.$$ The constants are independent of the polygon and the mesh, provided the determining neighborhoods have the boundary geometries and chart properties specified above. For sufficiently small $\theta$, the boundary moments remain uniformly small and tend to zero as the scale index tends to infinity, uniformly over all the finite runs.
  - Explanation: Boundary moments obey a recurrence with a strict linear contraction plus bulk and quadratic terms, so they stay small and decay uniformly over the finite runs.
- **claim-cap-change** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cap-change)): Let $p$ be the probability of $E_{\mathrm P}$ in a four-change open-cap law with arbitrary positive FK edge odds. Under separate primal closure, the probability of an actual primal crossing is $$\label{eq:cap-change} \mathcal G(p)=\frac{p}{p+\sqrt2(1-p)}.$$ The same formula holds for conditional probabilities after any exploration history that reveals only encountered switches. The density between the two full-configuration laws and its reciprocal are bounded by absolute constants. The analogous statement holds with primal and dual interchanged.
  - Explanation: An exact rational change of measure converts the pairing probability in the open-cap law into the actual crossing probability under separate closure, also conditionally on explored histories, with bounded densities.
- **claim-pure-four-mark** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-pure-four-mark)): Let $P$ be a fixed collared orthogonal polygon that is a topological disk in an oriented flat surface with a locally injective translation projection to the plane. Put four distinct marks in straight boundary portions. Approximate $P$ by its lifted square grid at mesh $\delta\downarrow0$, with critical odds $\sqrt2$ on every edge. Then the open-cap probability of $E_{\mathrm P}$ tends to $f(\chi_P)$, and the probability of an actual crossing between separately wired arcs $ab$ and $cd$ tends to $g(\chi_P)$. The conclusion also holds for compatible medial approximations differing by an incident boundary layer.
  - Explanation: For critical odds on a polygon in a flat surface, the open-cap pairing and the separate-wiring crossing probabilities converge to explicit functions of the conformal cross-ratio, extending the planar four-mark observable theorem to non-injective projections.
- **claim-fixed-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-fixed-tests)): For each fixed polygon $P$ in Proposition <a href="#prop:pure-four-mark" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:pure-four-mark">13</a>, assign to every lifted edge the random odds of its projected edge. At the critical inverse temperature of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the separately wired actual crossing probability tends in environmental probability to $g(\chi_P)$. The open-cap probability tends to $f(\chi_P)$.
  - Explanation: Combining the disorder comparison with the pure limit, quenched crossing and open-cap probabilities on each fixed polygon converge in environmental probability to the same conformal limits.
- **claim-compatible-drawing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-compatible-drawing)): The exploration in a filled lattice domain has a tile-and-collar drawing in a Jordan polygon $V_n$ with the following properties.* 1) *The marked boundary of $V_n$ has the same uniform Jordan limit as that of $D_n$.* 2) *The drawn strand $\widehat\gamma_n$ is simple, follows the same ordered local pieces as $\gamma_n$, and satisfies $d_{\mathrm{curv}}(\widehat\gamma_n,\gamma_n)=O(\delta_n)$.* 3) *After a switch-revealing stop, the remaining domain is a simple slit disk. Under deterministic cap closure the unknown edges have their original FK odds, with the revealed open edges contracted and revealed closed edges deleted.* 4) *Each bank of the prefix has physical access, within $O(\delta_n)$, to its corresponding known edges and boundary wire. Every completion embeds its actual primal and dual paths without crossing the past. No uncertain physical edge is duplicated.* *The same drawing and caps can be used away from an interval at which two extra boundary changes are inserted.
  - Explanation: The exploration admits a simple drawing in a Jordan polygon that stays close to the original curve, retains the original conditional FK odds after revealing switches, and gives each bank physical access to known edges.
- **claim-quenched-diagonal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-quenched-diagonal)): From every subsequence of the prescribed meshes one can extract a further deterministic subsequence such that, for $\mathbb P_{\rm env}$-almost every environment, the following hold:* 1. *every test in $\mathcal T$ has the pure limiting crossing probability of Proposition <a href="#prop:pure-four-mark" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:pure-four-mark">13</a>;* 2. *every fixed annulus in the second collection is eventually good.
  - Explanation: A Borel–Cantelli diagonal argument yields a deterministic subsequence of meshes along which all countably many polygon tests converge and all countably many annulus barriers eventually hold, for almost every environment.
- **claim-full-grid-arm** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-full-grid-arm)): For every fixed $R>0$ and bounded set $K\subset\mathbb C$, $$\label{eq:full-grid-arm} \lim_{r\downarrow0}\limsup_{n\to\infty} \sup_{z\in K} \phi^{\rm w}_{n,z;r,R}\bigl(\mathcal R_n(z;r,R)\bigr)=0.$$ The same conclusion holds for actual arms in subgraphs of the annulus whose induced boundary partition has no wire attachments away from the rims, apart from ordinary identifications at the same physical vertex. It also bounds the event that an arm occurs in any of a collection of such subgraphs, when all are dominated on their shared edges by one full-grid annulus.
  - Explanation: Radial arm probabilities across fixed physical annuli vanish as the inner radius shrinks after the mesh limit, uniformly over centers in a bounded set, and the bound extends to arms in subgraphs dominated by the full-grid annulus.
- **claim-macroscopic-avoidability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-macroscopic-avoidability)): For every fixed outer radius $R>0$, the conditional probability of a future unforced crossing of $A(z;r,R)$ tends to zero as $r\downarrow0$ after the mesh limit, uniformly over revealing histories and centers in a bounded set. The assertion remains true if the stop occurs during a revealed local arc.
  - Explanation: Future unforced annular crossings, that is crossings by components not separating tip from target, have conditional probability vanishing as the inner radius shrinks, uniformly over revealing histories.
- **claim-curve-tightness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-curve-tightness)): For every $\rho>0$, the variables $N_\rho(\widehat\gamma_n)$ are stochastically bounded. This holds for Dobrushin strands and for four-change strands with four fixed separated limiting marks, in either deterministic separate closure or open-cap normalization. It also holds for all their stopped prefixes. Their laws are tight in the uniform oriented-curve topology modulo increasing reparametrization, both in physical coordinates and in the common disk coordinates.
  - Explanation: The number of macroscopic displacements of the drawn strands and of their stopped prefixes is stochastically bounded, which gives tightness in the oriented-curve topology.
- **claim-tip-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-tip-approximation)): For every $T,R<\infty$ and $\varepsilon>0$, $$\label{eq:tip-approximation} \lim_{y\downarrow0}\limsup_{n\to\infty} \mathbb P\left(\sup_{0\le t\le\tau_n(T,R)} \|H_n(t,y)-\eta_n(t)\|>\varepsilon\right)=0.$$
  - Explanation: The curve tip is uniformly approximated by an interior point of the inverse Loewner map at small positive height, with probability of failure vanishing in the stated order of limits.
- **claim-no-return** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-no-return)): Let $\theta_n(r)$ be the first time $\eta_n$ enters the disk cap $\mathbb D\cap B(1,r)$. For every $b>0$, $$\lim_{r\downarrow0}\limsup_{n\to\infty} \mathbb P\bigl(\operatorname{diam} \eta_n[\theta_n(r),\infty)>b\bigr)=0.$$
  - Explanation: After first entering a small cap at the target, the curve makes a macroscopic excursion afterwards with probability tending to zero in the stated order of limits.
- **claim-moving-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-moving-test)): Consider the four-change open-cap law and any sequence of switch-revealing stopping rules before completion of its strand. Let $A_n$ be events determined by the stopped history. Suppose that, on $A_n$, there are basepoints $z_n$ and constants $r_0,\alpha_0>0$, independent of $n$ and of the history, such that* 1. *$B(z_n,r_0)\subset U_n$;* 2. *in the conformal disk chart normalized at $z_n$, the four current prime-end marks, including the tip, have pairwise distance at least $\alpha_0$ on the unit circle.* *Assume also that the stopped prefixes form a tight family in the oriented-curve topology, as in Proposition <a href="#prop:curve-tightness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:curve-tightness">19</a>. Write $\mathcal F_n$ for the stopped information, $E_{\mathrm P}$ for the primal pairing, and $\chi_{U_n}$ for the ordered cross-ratio of the four current marks. Then, for every $t>0$, $$\label{eq:slits-moving-test} \mathbb P_{\mathrm{open}}\left( A_n\cap\left\{ \left\|\mathbb P_{\mathrm{open}}(E_{\mathrm P}\mid\mathcal F_n) -f(\chi_{U_n})\right\|>t\right\}\right)\longrightarrow0.$$ The basepoints may be selected from the history. The original domains lie in a fixed bounded set, so every deterministic sequence of these basepoints has a convergent subsequence.
  - Explanation: After exploration, the conditional open-cap pairing probability is asymptotically the pure four-mark function of the cross-ratio of the remaining slit domain, under non-degeneracy of marks and tightness of prefixes.
- **claim-slits-chart** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slits-chart)): Under the base-disk and mark-separation assumptions above, there is a common modulus $\omega(r)\downarrow0$ such that the oscillation of $u_n$ on any connected path in $U_n$ of physical diameter at most $r$ is at most $\omega(r)$. The statement includes prime-end accesses by interior approximation.* *Consequently, for every $\beta>0$, a possible connection starting in $$\label{eq:slits-wide-band} B_n(\beta)=\{\beta\leq u_n\leq1-\beta\}$$ must travel a fixed positive physical distance before reaching an electrode or a known open bank edge of the tested color, for all sufficiently small meshes. The distance is uniform in the history.
  - Explanation: Small physical paths in the remaining domain have uniformly small oscillation in the rectangle coordinate, so central possible connections must travel a fixed distance to reach an electrode.
- **claim-slits-deletion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slits-deletion)): Fix a history and use separate closure for the tested color. Delete a set $S$ of unrevealed edges. The change in the probability of an actual electrode-to-electrode connection is at most the original conditional probability that an endpoint of an edge in $S$ has an actual connection to an electrode, allowing one edge of $S$ as an initial test step.* *If the deleted incidences lie in $B_n(\beta)$ and in a union of $m$ balls of radius $r$, where $m$ is fixed, this cost tends to zero uniformly in their centers and in the histories, in the order $$\lim_{r\downarrow0}\limsup_{n\to\infty}.$$ The same conclusion holds with a fixed number of nested bands and slightly enlarged balls.
  - Explanation: Deleting unrevealed edges changes the crossing probability at most by the chance that a deleted endpoint connects to an electrode, and this cost is small for deletions inside a fixed number of small balls in a central band.
- **claim-slits-fibers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slits-fibers)): For every prescribed physical tail radius $r>0$, one can choose a simple top-to-bottom fiber in each bin, and pass to a subsequence, with the following properties.* 1. *Each fiber stays in its bin in rectangle coordinates. Apart from a top and a bottom tail, it converges inside a compact subset of $U$. Each tail is contained in a physical ball of radius $r$.* 2. *All physical endpoints of all the fibers have distinct limits.* 3. *The fibers are mutually disjoint and ordered by their bins.* *The omitted top and bottom portions may have fixed, sufficiently small fractions of the rectangle height. The two half-fibers may use different abscissas within the same bin.
  - Explanation: One can choose disjoint ordered top-to-bottom fibers in prescribed abscissa bins whose tails are physically small and whose endpoints have distinct limits.
- **claim-slits-cover** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slits-cover)): By choosing the finite boundary sample sufficiently fine, the disk charts of $\widetilde O_n$ on the retained fiber cores and any fixed compact access paths in $U$ can be made arbitrarily close to those of $U_n$, for all sufficiently large $n$.* *Give $\widetilde O_n$ rectangle coordinates using the same four disk angles as for $U_n$, and denote their horizontal coordinate by $v_n$. After the tail radius and chart error have been chosen sufficiently small relative to the bin spacing, each lifted fiber is a crosscut of the cover rectangle, joining its horizontal sides and lying in the corresponding abscissa bin with an arbitrarily small enlargement. Consequently $$\label{eq:slits-chart-matching} \|v_n\circ\iota_n-u_n\|\leq C\lambda$$ between the two extreme fibers. If $\Gamma_n^-$ and $\Gamma_n^+$ are the first and last fibers, respectively, then $$\begin{aligned} v_n(\iota_n(w))&\leq \sup_{\iota_n(\Gamma_n^-)}v_n &&\text{on the left electrode side of }\Gamma_n^-,\\ v_n(\iota_n(w))&\geq \inf_{\iota_n(\Gamma_n^+)}v_n &&\text{on the right electrode side of }\Gamma_n^+. \end{aligned}$$ In particular, the enlarged extreme bins bound these coordinates strictly outside the future test cuts at $a_*$ and $1-a_*$.
  - Explanation: Charts of the universal cover of the punctured domain approximate those of the remaining domain on retained cores, and lifted fibers become crosscuts that trap coordinates globally.
- **claim-slits-compact-candidates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slits-compact-candidates)): After these deletions, all candidate incidences in $B_n(5\beta)$ lift into a fixed compact part of the limiting cover charts, for all sufficiently large $n$.
  - Explanation: After deleting edges near anchor tails and punctures, all central candidate incidences lift into a fixed compact part of the cover, via access paths with a bounded number of controlled displacements.
- **claim-slits-fixed-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slits-fixed-test)): The polygon just constructed may be chosen from the countable stock in Lemma <a href="#lem:quenched-diagonal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:quenched-diagonal">16</a>. For all sufficiently large $n$, its rounded graph on the varying cover uses the same finite system of physical-coordinate charts and the same incidence data. Its quenched crossing probability therefore converges to the pure four-mark value for this fixed polygon.
  - Explanation: The comparison polygon can be taken from the fixed countable stock, so its quenched crossing probability converges to the pure four-mark value without a rate over varying polygons.
- **claim-slits-shared** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slits-shared)): For sufficiently small geometric errors and sufficiently large $n$, $$\label{eq:slits-shared-comparison} \phi_{H_n}(\text{actual original-electrode crossing}) \leq \phi_{Q_n}^{\mathrm{sep}}(\text{test-electrode crossing}),$$ where $\phi_{H_n}$ is the conditional FK law and the two test electrodes are wired separately in $\phi_{Q_n}^{\mathrm{sep}}$.
  - Explanation: On shared candidate edges, the conditional crossing probability of the remaining domain is bounded above by the crossing probability of the fixed test with separately wired electrodes.
- **claim-bounded-martingale-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bounded-martingale-tests)): For each $x>2R+1$, every sequence of revealing stopping times $0\le\sigma_n\le\upsilon_n\le\tau_n$, and every uniformly bounded $\mathcal F_{n,\sigma_n}$-measurable sequence $F_n$, the original Dobrushin law satisfies $$\label{eq:approximate-martingale} \mathbb E_n\left[ F_n\bigl(M_{n,\upsilon_n}(x)-M_{n,\sigma_n}(x)\bigr) \right]\longrightarrow0.$$ Stops during a known local arc are allowed.
  - Explanation: Bounded power-type Loewner test functions are approximate martingales for the original Dobrushin law, obtained by inserting a short wired interval and comparing laws only after bounding the tests.
- **claim-driving-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-driving-convergence)): In the fixed environment under consideration, $W_n$ converges in law, uniformly on compact capacity intervals, to $\sqrt{16/3}\,B$, where $B$ is standard real Brownian motion.
  - Explanation: In a fixed good environment the Loewner drivers converge in law, uniformly on compact capacity intervals, to a rescaled standard Brownian motion; two test points identify drift and quadratic variation.

The convergence is uniform in $x$, and the event that <a href=\"#eq:barrier-input\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:barrier-input\">[eq:barrier-input]</a> holds depends only on the bonds of the annulus and its incident lattice layer. The same bound holds for every less wired boundary condition. The assertion also holds for dual crossings with dual odds.",
      "explanation": "Imported local barrier: at the critical point a radial open crossing of a lattice annulus with wired rims has probability bounded away from one, with environmental probability tending to one; no rate is given.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-bulk-input",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed support constant $C_{\\rm s}$ with the following properties. Choose a sufficiently large odd step factor $L$, a sufficiently small flag threshold parameter $b_0>0$ thereafter, and a sufficiently large fixed $s_0$. For all sufficiently small $\\theta>0$, the plane fields <a href=\"#eq:initial-logs\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:initial-logs\">[eq:initial-logs]</a> at $h=0$ admit successive transformations to potentials $f_i^{(k)}$ at spacings $s_k=s_0L^k$ such that:*\n\n1.  *Each $f_i^{(k)}$ is a bounded real spin-flip-even function, is centered by $\\mathbb E_0 f_i^{(k)}=0$, and has field support and environmental dependence in the square of radius $C_{\\rm s}s_k$ about $s_ki$. The primitive environmental variables are independent. Collections with disjoint dependence neighborhoods are independent as random functions. The plane field is stationary on its grid and square symmetric in distribution.*\n\n2.  *With the fixed moment order $P=40$, $$X_k=\\bigl(\\mathbb E\\left\\lVert f_i^{(k)}\\right\\rVert_\\infty^P\\bigr)^{1/P}\n     \\longrightarrow0.\n     \\label{eq:bulk-decay}$$ The numbers $X_k$ are uniformly arbitrarily small when $\\theta$ is sufficiently small. The disorder bound and construction parameters are chosen independently of any domain or mesh approximation.*\n\n\n3.  *Every local replacement preserves exterior-integrated weights exactly, up to field-independent centering factors. This holds for arbitrary assignments of the old exterior field whenever the complete local construction is performed. For numerical $K_{\\rm s}$ and a constant $C_L$, one scale step satisfies $$\\left\\lVert f'_j\\right\\rVert_\\infty\\le\n     C_L\\sum_{i:\\,\\left\\lVert si-Lsj\\right\\rVert_\\infty\\le K_{\\rm s}Ls}\n              \\left\\lVert f_i\\right\\rVert_\\infty.\n     \\label{eq:map-raw-input}$$ The formulas are local and agree in embedded plane and torus charts.*\n\n*Any additional fixed lower bound on $L$, upper bound on $b_0$, or lower bound on $s_0$ can be imposed in this order, followed by decreasing the allowed disorder interval.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.3"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $d_0>1$ and $C<\\infty$ with the following properties for the pure shell kernels just defined.*\n\n\n1.  *At a straight side, if both rays are pinned plus, then for every fixed $d=R/r\\ge d_0$, $$\\Delta(T)\\le C d^{-2}.\n     \\label{eq:straight-boundary-transfer}$$ If both rays are free, the same bound holds for $\\overline T$.*\n\n2.  *At a corner with both rays pinned plus or both rays free, there is a fixed ratio $d_0$ for which the appropriate kernel has projective diameter at most $C$. The same finite bound holds at a straight boundary-condition change, with one ray pinned plus and the other free.*\n\n*In each statement the mesh is sufficiently fine relative to $r$. The threshold may depend on the fixed ratio and the finite collection of cap shapes. The constants are uniform under translation, lattice rounding, and bounded lattice-layer displacements of the cuts and dual boundaries.",

        "anchor": "stmt-cor-boundary-observables",
        "exact_quote": "Let a spin patch of diameter at most $r$ be separated from prescribed remote spins $\\zeta$ by pure shells of one of the types in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:boundary-transfer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:boundary-transfer\">6</a>, out to distance $R=dr$. Allow fixed proportional buffers around the patch and the outer cut. Let $H$ be a bounded real function of the patch spins and of any independent auxiliary variables in the patch. In the free/free case assume that the region inside the shell has no pinned spins and that $H$ is invariant under reversal of all its spin arguments. There are constants $C<\\infty$ and $\\eta>0$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{osc}}_\\zeta\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm{pure}}[H\\mid\\zeta]\n       &\\le C a(d)\\|H\\|_\\infty,\n       \\label{eq:boundary-expectation-transfer}\\\\\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{osc}}_\\zeta\n        \\log\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm{pure}}[e^H\\mid\\zeta]\n       &\\le C a(d)\\|H\\|_\\infty,\n       \\label{eq:boundary-log-transfer}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $a(d)=d^{-2}$ at a straight homogeneous side and $a(d)=d^{-\\eta}$ at a corner or boundary-condition change. Here $\\mathop{\\mathrm{osc}} f=\\sup f-\\inf f$. The bounds hold for each fixed sufficiently large $d$ and sufficiently fine mesh, with thresholds chosen simultaneously for any finite collection of ratios.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-polygon-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "There exists $\\theta_0>0$ with the following property. Fix $0<\\theta<\\theta_0$, a collared orthogonal polygon $\\mathcal P$, and four distinct boundary marks in straight side interiors. Wire each of two opposite marked boundary arcs separately and leave the intervening arcs free. On the rounded lifted grid assign odds $$v_e=\\sqrt2\\exp(\\theta\\xi_e),$$ where the physical-edge signs are independent and symmetric, and repeated lifted edges have the same sign. Let $\\phi_{\\delta,J}^{\\rm sep}$ and $\\phi_{\\delta,0}^{\\rm sep}$ denote respectively this random-cluster law and the pure law on the same graph and with the same boundary partition. If $\\mathcal A_\\delta$ is the event of an actual open path between the two wired arcs, then $$\\label{eq:polygon-comparison}\n \\phi_{\\delta,J}^{\\rm sep}(\\mathcal A_\\delta)\n -\\phi_{\\delta,0}^{\\rm sep}(\\mathcal A_\\delta)\n \\longrightarrow0\n \\qquad\\text{in environmental probability as }\\delta\\downarrow0.$$ The disorder threshold is independent of the fixed polygon and marks. The conclusion also holds on the dual square grid.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-polygon-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "There exists $\\theta_0>0$ with the following property. Let $\\phi_{\\delta,J}^{\\rm sep}$ and $\\phi_{\\delta,0}^{\\rm sep}$ denote respectively this random-cluster law and the pure law on the same graph and with the same boundary partition.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-exactness",
        "exact_quote": "For the terminal scale chosen above, the local construction is exact for each fixed environment and realization of its priorities and thresholds. Its potentials have support and environmental dependence radius at most $Cs_k$. Outside the boundary cells they are the plane potentials, or zero outside the pad. The two pin assignments admit the same scalar subtractions. More precisely, there is a scalar $a_\\delta$, independent of the pin assignment, such that $$\\label{eq:polygon-exact-partition}\n \\frac{\\mathcal Z_J^\\eta}{\\mathcal Z_0^\\eta}\n =e^{a_\\delta}\\mathbb E_0^\\eta\n       \\exp\\!\\left(\\sum_i f_i^{(N),\\eta}\\right),\n \\qquad \\eta\\in\\{++, +-\\}.$$ The initial boundary norms are uniformly $O_{s_0}(\\theta)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-exactness",
          "exact_quote": "For the terminal scale chosen above, the local construction is exact for each fixed environment and realization of its priorities and thresholds.           "source_id": "PM-223.3"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For the terminal scale chosen above, the local construction is exact for each fixed environment and realization of its priorities and thresholds.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-recursion",
        "exact_quote": "The scale-step parameters can be chosen in the order: $L$ sufficiently large, $b_0$ sufficiently small, and $s_0$ sufficiently large, so that for all $k<N$, $$\\label{eq:boundary-recurrence}\n Z_{k+1}\\le q_* Z_k+C_LX_k\n                    +C_{L,b_0}(Z_k+X_k)^2,\n \\qquad q_*<1.$$ The constants are independent of the polygon and the mesh, provided the determining neighborhoods have the boundary geometries and chart properties specified above. For sufficiently small $\\theta$, the boundary moments remain uniformly small and tend to zero as the scale index tends to infinity, uniformly over all the finite runs.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-recursion",
          "exact_quote": "The scale-step parameters can be chosen in the order: $L$ sufficiently large, $b_0$ sufficiently small, and $s_0$ sufficiently large, so that for all $k<N$, $$\\label{eq:boundary-recurrence}\n Z_{k+1}\\le q_* Z_k+C_LX_k\n                    +C_{L,b_0}(Z_k+X_k)^2,\n \\qquad q_*<1.$$ The constants are independent of the polygon and the mesh, provided the determining neighborhoods have the boundary geometries and chart properties specified above. For sufficiently small $\\theta$, the boundary moments remain uniformly small and tend to zero as the scale index tends to infinity, uniformly over all the finite runs.",
          "source_id": "PM-223.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The scale-step parameters can be chosen in the order: $L$ sufficiently large, $b_0$ sufficiently small, and $s_0$ sufficiently large, so that for all $k<N$, $$\\label{eq:boundary-recurrence}\n Z_{k+1}\\le q_* Z_k+C_LX_k\n                    +C_{L,b_0}(Z_k+X_k)^2,\n \\qquad q_*<1.$$ The constants are independent of the polygon and the mesh, provided the determining neighborhoods have the boundary geometries and chart properties specified above.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cap-change",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p$ be the probability of $E_{\\mathrm P}$ in a four-change open-cap law with arbitrary positive FK edge odds. Under separate primal closure, the probability of an actual primal crossing is $$\\label{eq:cap-change}\n \\mathcal G(p)=\\frac{p}{p+\\sqrt2(1-p)}.$$ The same formula holds for conditional probabilities after any exploration history that reveals only encountered switches. The density between the two full-configuration laws and its reciprocal are bounded by absolute constants. The analogous statement holds with primal and dual interchanged.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.3"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-pure-four-mark",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be a fixed collared orthogonal polygon that is a topological disk in an oriented flat surface with a locally injective translation projection to the plane. Put four distinct marks in straight boundary portions. Approximate $P$ by its lifted square grid at mesh $\\delta\\downarrow0$, with critical odds $\\sqrt2$ on every edge. Then the open-cap probability of $E_{\\mathrm P}$ tends to $f(\\chi_P)$, and the probability of an actual crossing between separately wired arcs $ab$ and $cd$ tends to $g(\\chi_P)$. The conclusion also holds for compatible medial approximations differing by an incident boundary layer.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-compatible-drawing",
        "exact_quote": "The exploration in a filled lattice domain has a tile-and-collar drawing in a Jordan polygon $V_n$ with the following properties.*\n\n1)  *The marked boundary of $V_n$ has the same uniform Jordan limit as that of $D_n$.*\n\n2)  *The drawn strand $\\widehat\\gamma_n$ is simple, follows the same ordered local pieces as $\\gamma_n$, and satisfies $d_{\\mathrm{curv}}(\\widehat\\gamma_n,\\gamma_n)=O(\\delta_n)$.*\n\n3)  *After a switch-revealing stop, the remaining domain is a simple slit disk. Under deterministic cap closure the unknown edges have their original FK odds, with the revealed open edges contracted and revealed closed edges deleted.*\n\n4)  *Each bank of the prefix has physical access, within $O(\\delta_n)$, to its corresponding known edges and boundary wire. Every completion embeds its actual primal and dual paths without crossing the past. No uncertain physical edge is duplicated.*\n\n*The same drawing and caps can be used away from an interval at which two extra boundary changes are inserted.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Every completion embeds its actual primal and dual paths without crossing the past.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-quenched-diagonal",
        "exact_quote": "From every subsequence of the prescribed meshes one can extract a further deterministic subsequence such that, for $\\mathbb P_{\\rm env}$-almost every environment, the following hold:*\n\n1.  *every test in $\\mathcal T$ has the pure limiting crossing probability of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:pure-four-mark\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:pure-four-mark\">13</a>;*\n\n2.  *every fixed annulus in the second collection is eventually good.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-moving-test",
        "exact_quote": "Consider the four-change open-cap law and any sequence of switch-revealing stopping rules before completion of its strand. Let $A_n$ be events determined by the stopped history. Suppose that, on $A_n$, there are basepoints $z_n$ and constants $r_0,\\alpha_0>0$, independent of $n$ and of the history, such that*\n\n1.  *$B(z_n,r_0)\\subset U_n$;*\n\n2.  *in the conformal disk chart normalized at $z_n$, the four current prime-end marks, including the tip, have pairwise distance at least $\\alpha_0$ on the unit circle.*\n\n\n*Assume also that the stopped prefixes form a tight family in the oriented-curve topology, as in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:curve-tightness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:curve-tightness\">19</a>. Write $\\mathcal F_n$ for the stopped information, $E_{\\mathrm P}$ for the primal pairing, and $\\chi_{U_n}$ for the ordered cross-ratio of the four current marks. Then, for every $t>0$, $$\\label{eq:slits-moving-test}\n \\mathbb P_{\\mathrm{open}}\\left(\n A_n\\cap\\left\\{\n \\left|\\mathbb P_{\\mathrm{open}}(E_{\\mathrm P}\\mid\\mathcal F_n)\n             -f(\\chi_{U_n})\\right|>t\\right\\}\\right)\\longrightarrow0.$$ The basepoints may be selected from the history. The original domains lie in a fixed bounded set, so every deterministic sequence of these basepoints has a convergent subsequence.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-slits-chart",
        "exact_quote": "Under the base-disk and mark-separation assumptions above, there is a common modulus $\\omega(r)\\downarrow0$ such that the oscillation of $u_n$ on any connected path in $U_n$ of physical diameter at most $r$ is at most $\\omega(r)$. The statement includes prime-end accesses by interior approximation.*\n\n*Consequently, for every $\\beta>0$, a possible connection starting in $$\\label{eq:slits-wide-band}\n B_n(\\beta)=\\{\\beta\\leq u_n\\leq1-\\beta\\}$$ must travel a fixed positive physical distance before reaching an electrode or a known open bank edge of the tested color, for all sufficiently small meshes. The distance is uniform in the history.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.3"
      },

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-slits-deletion",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a history and use separate closure for the tested color. Delete a set $S$ of unrevealed edges. The change in the probability of an actual electrode-to-electrode connection is at most the original conditional probability that an endpoint of an edge in $S$ has an actual connection to an electrode, allowing one edge of $S$ as an initial test step.*\n\n*If the deleted incidences lie in $B_n(\\beta)$ and in a union of $m$ balls of radius $r$, where $m$ is fixed, this cost tends to zero uniformly in their centers and in the histories, in the order $$\\lim_{r\\downarrow0}\\limsup_{n\\to\\infty}.$$ The same conclusion holds with a fixed number of nested bands and slightly enlarged balls.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-slits-deletion",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a history and use separate closure for the tested color. Delete a set $S$ of unrevealed edges.           "source_id": "PM-223.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix a history and use separate closure for the tested color. Delete a set $S$ of unrevealed edges.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-slits-fibers",
        "exact_quote": "For every prescribed physical tail radius $r>0$, one can choose a simple top-to-bottom fiber in each bin, and pass to a subsequence, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Each fiber stays in its bin in rectangle coordinates. Apart from a top and a bottom tail, it converges inside a compact subset of $U$. Each tail is contained in a physical ball of radius $r$.*\n\n2.  *All physical endpoints of all the fibers have distinct limits.*\n\n3.  *The fibers are mutually disjoint and ordered by their bins.*\n\n*The omitted top and bottom portions may have fixed, sufficiently small fractions of the rectangle height. The two half-fibers may use different abscissas within the same bin.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-slits-cover",
        "exact_quote": "By choosing the finite boundary sample sufficiently fine, the disk charts of $\\widetilde O_n$ on the retained fiber cores and any fixed compact access paths in $U$ can be made arbitrarily close to those of $U_n$, for all sufficiently large $n$.*\n\n\n*Give $\\widetilde O_n$ rectangle coordinates using the same four disk angles as for $U_n$, and denote their horizontal coordinate by $v_n$. After the tail radius and chart error have been chosen sufficiently small relative to the bin spacing, each lifted fiber is a crosscut of the cover rectangle, joining its horizontal sides and lying in the corresponding abscissa bin with an arbitrarily small enlargement. Consequently $$\\label{eq:slits-chart-matching}\n |v_n\\circ\\iota_n-u_n|\\leq C\\lambda$$ between the two extreme fibers. If $\\Gamma_n^-$ and $\\Gamma_n^+$ are the first and last fibers, respectively, then $$\\begin{aligned}\n v_n(\\iota_n(w))&\\leq\n     \\sup_{\\iota_n(\\Gamma_n^-)}v_n\n       &&\\text{on the left electrode side of }\\Gamma_n^-,\\\\\n v_n(\\iota_n(w))&\\geq\n     \\inf_{\\iota_n(\\Gamma_n^+)}v_n\n       &&\\text{on the right electrode side of }\\Gamma_n^+.\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, the enlarged extreme bins bound these coordinates strictly outside the future test cuts at $a_*$ and $1-a_*$.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-slits-cover",
          "exact_quote": "By choosing the finite boundary sample sufficiently fine, the disk charts of $\\widetilde O_n$ on the retained fiber cores and any fixed compact access paths in $U$ can be made arbitrarily close to those of $U_n$, for all sufficiently large $n$.*\n\n\n*Give $\\widetilde O_n$ rectangle coordinates using the same four disk angles as for $U_n$, and denote their horizontal coordinate by $v_n$. Consequently $$\\label{eq:slits-chart-matching}\n |v_n\\circ\\iota_n-u_n|\\leq C\\lambda$$ between the two extreme fibers.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bounded-martingale-tests",
        "exact_quote": "For each $x>2R+1$, every sequence of revealing stopping times $0\\le\\sigma_n\\le\\upsilon_n\\le\\tau_n$, and every uniformly bounded $\\mathcal F_{n,\\sigma_n}$-measurable sequence $F_n$, the original Dobrushin law satisfies $$\\label{eq:approximate-martingale}\n \\mathbb E_n\\left[\n F_n\\bigl(M_{n,\\upsilon_n}(x)-M_{n,\\sigma_n}(x)\\bigr)\n \\right]\\longrightarrow0.$$ Stops during a known local arc are allowed.",
        "source_id": "PM-223.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-bounded-martingale-tests",
          "exact_quote": "For each $x>2R+1$, every sequence of revealing stopping times $0\\le\\sigma_n\\le\\upsilon_n\\le\\tau_n$, and every uniformly bounded $\\mathcal F_{n,\\sigma_n}$-measurable sequence $F_n$, the original Dobrushin law satisfies $$\\label{eq:approximate-martingale}\n \\mathbb E_n\\left[\n F_n\\bigl(M_{n,\\upsilon_n}(x)-M_{n,\\sigma_n}(x)\\bigr)\n \\right]\\longrightarrow0.$$ Stops during a known local arc are allowed.",
          "source_id": "PM-223.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For each $x>2R+1$, every sequence of revealing stopping times $0\\le\\sigma_n\\le\\upsilon_n\\le\\tau_n$, and every uniformly bounded $\\mathcal F_{n,\\sigma_n}$-measurable sequence $F_n$, the original Dobrushin law satisfies $$\\label{eq:approximate-martingale}\n \\mathbb E_n\\left[\n F_n\\bigl(M_{n,\\upsilon_n}(x)-M_{n,\\sigma_n}(x)\\bigr)\n \\right]\\longrightarrow0.$$ Stops during a known local arc are allowed.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **projective diameter** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): For a strictly positive kernel $T$ on two finite state spaces, its *projective diameter* is $$\Delta(T)=\sup_{u,u',v,v'} \left\|\log\frac{T(u,v)T(u',v')}{T(u,v')T(u',v)}\right\|. \label{eq:projective-diameter}$$ If both boundary rays are free, write $[u]=\{u,-u\}$ and define the kernel on cap states modulo sign by $$\overline T([u],[v]) =\sum_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon'\in\{-1,1\}} T(\varepsilon u,\varepsilon'v). \label{eq:quotient-shell-kernel}$$ Thus the two absolute cap signs are summed independently.
- **collared orthogonal polygon** ([source](#stmt-def-sheet-polygon)): Let $\mathcal S$ be an oriented flat surface with a map $\pi:\mathcal S\to\mathbb R^2$ whose restriction to each sufficiently small neighborhood is an orientation-preserving isometry. A *collared orthogonal polygon* is a compact polygonal disk $\mathcal P\subset \mathcal S$ such that $\pi$ is defined on a neighborhood of $\mathcal P$, and each side of $\mathcal P$ is horizontal or vertical in these local coordinates. The polygon has finitely many sides; the map $\pi$ need not be injective on $\mathcal P$. Its lattice approximation is obtained by rounding its sides to the lifted square grid of mesh $\delta$ and filling the resulting polygon. Boundary-condition changes are placed in straight side interiors and rounded with the sides. Distinct lifted copies of a physical lattice edge are distinct random-cluster edges with the same environmental sign. Thus the coupling of a lifted edge $\widetilde e$ is the coupling of $\pi(\widetilde e)$.
- **pure critical coupling** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Let $K_0=\frac12\log(1+\sqrt2)$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-sle-variance-driver | scaling of the limiting driver (multiple of standard Brownian motion) | = | $16/3$ | the limiting driver is the square root of this value times standard Brownian motion |
| q-moment-order | fixed moment order of the bulk potentials | = | $40$ | moment order used in the decay of the potential norms |
| b-straight-boundary-diameter | projective diameter of the shell kernel at a straight side | ≤ | $C d^{-2}$ | scale ratio d at least a fixed threshold, mesh fine relative to the inner radius |
| q-cap-change-map | actual crossing probability as a function of the open-cap pairing probability | = | $\frac{p}{p+\sqrt2(1-p)}$ | p is the open-cap probability of the primal pairing |
| q-self-duality-product | product of the two shifted exponential odds at the critical temperature | = | $2$ | defines the critical inverse temperature as the unique positive solution |
| q-disordered-odds | random-cluster edge odds on the rounded lifted grid | = | $\sqrt2\exp(\theta\xi_e)$ | independent symmetric signs, repeated lifted edges share the sign |
| b-boundary-contraction | linear contraction factor in the boundary recurrence | < | $1$ | parameters chosen in the stated order, independent of polygon and mesh |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **r-critical** [claim](#stmt-prop-critical-input) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-critical-input): Import of the critical-point identification from the companion work, using normalized quenched magnetization. -> Critical temperature fixed by self-duality, with vanishing log-odds disorder.
- **r-barrier** [claim](#stmt-lem-barrier-input) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-barrier-input): Import of local FK barriers from the companion work, transferred to dual colour by duality. -> Annulus crossing probability bounded away from one with environmental probability tending to one.
- **r-bulk** [claim](#stmt-prop-bulk-input) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bulk-input): Import of the local scale map and small critical trajectory from the companion work. -> Bulk potentials with decaying moments and exact local replacements.
- **r-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-transfer): Supermodularity reduction, boundary Pfaffian identity for pinned rays, FK screening for free rays, arm comparison for a change of boundary condition. -> Projective diameter bounds for shell kernels.
- **r-observables** [claim](#stmt-cor-boundary-observables) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-boundary-observables): Relative-density comparison across shells, composition of contractions at corners. -> Oscillation bounds for conditional expectations and logarithms.
- **r-boundary-exact** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-exactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-exactness): Exact local replacements with a common scalar subtraction for both pin assignments, using spin-flip conjugacy. -> Partition-function ratio as a scalar times a pure expectation of terminal potentials.
- **r-boundary-recursion** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-recursion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-recursion): Linear transfer, Taylor remainder, independent-flag moment bound and isolated-group transfer, then a majorant argument. -> Uniform smallness and decay of boundary moments.
- **r-polygon** [claim](#stmt-prop-polygon-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-recursion-2): Edwards–Sokal tanh ratio, terminal potentials small in the mean, Lipschitz property of tanh. -> Crossing probability under disorder close to the pure one on a fixed polygon.
- **r-cap-change** [claim](#stmt-lem-cap-change) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cap-change): Loop-count density between separate closure and open-cap law. -> Exact map between pairing and crossing probabilities.
- **r-pure-four-mark** [claim](#stmt-prop-pure-four-mark) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pure-four-mark): Fermionic observable on the flat disk, compactness, boundary sign argument, identification of the slit strip, boundary-layer comparison. -> Pure four-point limits on sheet polygons.
- **r-fixed-tests** [claim](#stmt-cor-fixed-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-fixed-tests): Combine polygon comparison with the pure limit and invert the crossing map. -> Quenched limits of fixed tests.
- **r-drawing** [claim](#stmt-lem-compatible-drawing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compatible-drawing): Tile-and-collar construction with deterministic caps and Euler identity for component weights. -> Simple drawing preserving conditional FK laws.
- **r-diagonal** [claim](#stmt-lem-quenched-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quenched-diagonal): Enumerate tests and annuli, choose meshes with summable failure probabilities, apply Borel–Cantelli. -> Deterministic subsequence and almost sure environment.
- **r-arm** [claim](#stmt-lem-full-grid-arm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-full-grid-arm): Successive conditioning over separated good annuli with FK monotonicity. -> Vanishing radial arm probability at small inner radius.
- **r-avoidability** [claim](#stmt-lem-macroscopic-avoidability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-macroscopic-avoidability): Missing-colour argument on avoidable components, comparison to a full-grid annulus. -> Unforced crossings are unlikely.
- **r-tightness** [claim](#stmt-prop-curve-tightness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-curve-tightness): Territorial arm counting, whole-component exploration with geometric tails, finite cover, Arzelà–Ascoli. -> Stochastic boundedness of displacement counts and tightness.
- **r-tip** [claim](#stmt-prop-tip-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tip-approximation): Length-area crosscuts, Gehring–Hayman diameter bound, small-gate stopping rules and avoidability. -> Uniform tip approximation by interior points.
- **r-no-return** [claim](#stmt-lem-no-return) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-no-return): Stop at first entrance to the cap and apply avoidability. -> No macroscopic return from a small target cap.
- **r-slits-chart** [claim](#stmt-lem-slits-chart) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slits-chart): Brownian hitting estimates and the crosscut form of the Beurling estimate. -> Uniform modulus for the rectangle coordinate.
- **r-slits-deletion** [claim](#stmt-lem-slits-deletion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slits-deletion): Monotone coupling and cluster exploration from deleted endpoints, then a full-grid arm bound. -> Small cost of deleting edges near balls.
- **r-slits-fibers** [claim](#stmt-lem-slits-fibers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slits-fibers): Cauchy–Schwarz length-area estimate and harmonic-measure selection of endpoints. -> Disjoint ordered fibers with small tails.
- **r-slits-cover** [claim](#stmt-lem-slits-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slits-cover): Normal family, Hurwitz and Schwarz lemmas on the universal cover; crosscut separation. -> Cover charts matching the slit domain and global coordinate trapping.
- **r-slits-compact** [claim](#stmt-lem-slits-compact-candidates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slits-compact-candidates): Trimmed domain with bounded oscillation count of its boundary, access paths in finitely many charts. -> Central candidates lie in a compact part of the cover.
- **r-slits-fixed-test** [claim](#stmt-lem-slits-fixed-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slits-fixed-test): Describe the polygon by finitely many rational chart data from the countable stock. -> Comparison polygon is a fixed test.
- **r-slits-shared** [claim](#stmt-lem-slits-shared) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slits-shared): Condition on nonshared edges, dominate induced partition by separate wiring, apply FK monotonicity. -> Upper bound by the test crossing probability.
- **r-moving-test** [claim](#stmt-prop-moving-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slits-shared-2): Argument by contradiction on a failing history subsequence, choosing margins in a fixed order before the mesh limit. -> Conditional pairing probability matches the pure four-mark function.
- **r-martingale-tests** [claim](#stmt-lem-bounded-martingale-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bounded-martingale-tests): Insert a short wired interval, replace the pairing martingale by a bounded Loewner expression, then compare laws by coupling. -> Approximate martingale identities for power test functions.
- **r-driving** [claim](#stmt-prop-driving-convergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-driving-convergence): Aldous tightness from a convexity estimate with two test points, limit martingales, subtraction to kill drift, Lévy characterization. -> Driver converges to rescaled Brownian motion.
- **r-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-driving-convergence-2): Capacity-time convergence, tip approximation, SLE trace theorem, no-return bound, then subsequence criterion for convergence in environmental probability. -> Convergence of quenched interface laws to chordal SLE.

<!-- PM:END pm.roadmap.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-quenched-sle-limit**: For weak symmetric two-valued bond disorder at fixed strength below a threshold that does not depend on the domain, the conditional law of the FK-Ising exploration curve converges, in probability over the environment, to the law of chordal SLE with parameter 16/3.
- **inv-cap-change-formula**: In a four-change open-cap law with arbitrary positive edge odds, the probability of an actual primal crossing under separate primal closure is an explicit increasing function of the pairing probability.
- **inv-driver-brownian**: In a fixed environment, the Loewner driving processes of the explorations converge in law, uniformly on compact capacity intervals, to a Brownian motion with variance rate 16/3.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-mesh-rate**: Read the theorem as a quantitative error bound for a given mesh or as a guarantee for one realised disorder sample. -> The convergence is only in environmental probability and the paper provides no rate, so no finite-size tolerance follows.
- **anti-strong-disorder**: Apply the SLE limit to disorder strengths of arbitrary size. -> The statement is only made below a threshold, and nothing is claimed beyond it.
- **anti-other-disorder-law**: Transfer the conclusion to Gaussian, asymmetric or correlated bond disorder. -> The proofs use independent symmetric two-valued signs, including the self-duality at the critical point.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are asymptotic convergence statements in probability for random curves and drivers as the mesh tends to zero, proved by compactness, subsequence extraction and martingale identification. No finite procedure computes a bound or object from them, and the paper gives no rate, so there is nothing executable to certify.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-small-disorder** (hypothesis): The main convergence is asserted only for a fixed disorder strength below a threshold, and nothing is claimed for larger disorder. Blocked use: ['Applying the SLE limit to strong or frustrated bond disorder', 'Extending the result to disorder strengths above the threshold']
- **lim-environmental-probability** (formal_scope): The conclusion holds only as convergence in probability over the environment, with no rate. It is not almost sure convergence for a single realised environment. Blocked use: ['Claiming almost sure convergence for one disorder sample', 'Deriving a finite-mesh error bound from the theorem']
- **lim-disorder-law** (hypothesis): The couplings are built from independent symmetric two-valued random signs, and the critical point and barrier inputs rely on this law. Blocked use: ['Transferring the result to Gaussian, asymmetric or spatially correlated disorder', 'Using the pure critical temperature instead of the disordered critical one']
- **lim-critical-temperature** (hypothesis): The local barrier input is stated at the physical critical inverse temperature only, and the critical point identification needs sufficiently small disorder. Blocked use: ['Applying the barrier or interface limit at off-critical temperatures', 'Reading the barrier bound as holding with a rate in the environment']
- **lim-no-uniform-rate-polygons** (approximation): The fixed polygon comparison is a convergence in environmental probability for each fixed polygon. No uniform rate over polygons or exploration histories is proved, so the argument works only with a fixed countable stock of tests. Blocked use: ['Applying the polygon estimate to boundaries that change with the mesh', 'Using a union bound over all microscopic domains']
- **lim-fixed-physical-scales** (edge_case): The arm bounds hold for physical radii fixed before the mesh tends to zero, with the limit in the inner radius taken after the mesh limit. They give no estimate down to the lattice spacing. Blocked use: ['Using the arm bound at microscopic scales', 'Interchanging the order of the limits in the radius and the mesh']
- **lim-planar-ising** (formal_scope): The driver identification uses the cluster weight giving power one half and the planar Loewner structure, so the variance rate 16/3 is specific to this model. Blocked use: ['Reading the variance rate for other cluster weights', 'Applying the result to non-planar lattices']
- **lim-nonconstructive-extraction** (nonconstructive): The deterministic subsequence of meshes comes from a Borel-Cantelli diagonal choice, so no explicit mesh sequence is produced. Blocked use: ['Extracting an explicit mesh schedule or algorithm from the proof']

[stmt-prop-driving-convergence](#stmt-prop-driving-convergence) | proposition | `prop:driving-convergence` | 31 | In the fixed environment under consideration, $W_n$ converges in law, uniformly on compact capacity intervals, to $\\sqrt{16/3}\\,B$, where $B$ is standard real Brownian motion.* |\n\n\n### Introduction\n\nThe critical FK–Ising model describes the connectivity of the clusters in the Edwards–Sokal representation of the Ising model. Its planar exploration interface has the conformally invariant scaling limit $\\mathrm{SLE}_{16/3}$. We prove that this interface limit persists when each lattice coupling is perturbed by a small independent symmetric two-valued random variable, with the disorder held fixed while the interface is sampled.\n\n\n#### The model and the result\n\n\nLet $\\mathcal E$ be the nearest-neighbor edge set of $\\mathbb Z^2$, and let $\\xi=(\\xi_e)_{e\\in\\mathcal E}$ be independent random signs, each equally likely to be $-1$ or $1$. For $0<\\varepsilon<1$, put $$\\label{eq:random-couplings}\n J_e=1+\\varepsilon\\xi_e.$$ We write $\\mathbb P_{\\mathrm{env}}$ for the law of $\\xi$. The critical inverse temperature is the plus-state magnetization threshold $$\\beta_c(\\varepsilon)=\\inf\\bigl\\{\\beta>0:\n       \\mathbb E_{\\mathrm{env}}\\langle\\sigma_0\\rangle^+_{\\beta,J}>0\\bigr\\}.$$ Here the infinite-volume Gibbs expectation is normalized for each environment before taking the environmental expectation. This threshold also agrees with the almost-sure magnetization threshold: positivity propagates between neighboring vertices for strictly positive ferromagnetic couplings, so its occurrence is a translation-invariant environmental event. Ergodicity, monotonicity in $\\beta$, and a countable intersection over rational $\\beta$ give the equivalence.\n\n\nFix a bounded Jordan domain $D\\subset\\mathbb C$ and distinct $a,b\\in\\partial D$. A square-lattice domain is a finite filled lattice domain: all its interior nearest-neighbor edges are present. Boundary collars may separate incidences at a boundary vertex when recording the order of boundary arcs. Consider deterministic such domains $G_n=(V_n,E_n)$ at physical mesh $\\delta_n\\downarrow0$, with polygonal domains $D_n$ and marked boundary points $a_n,b_n$. Assume there are orientation-preserving boundary homeomorphisms $h_n:\\partial D\\to\\partial D_n$ such that $$\\label{eq:jordan-approximation}\n \\sup_{z\\in\\partial D}|h_n(z)-z|\\longrightarrow0,\n \\qquad a_n\\longrightarrow a,\\quad b_n\\longrightarrow b,$$ where the marked points correspond in boundary order. All coordinates in <a href=\"#eq:jordan-approximation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:jordan-approximation\">[eq:jordan-approximation]</a> are physical coordinates, including the factor $\\delta_n$.\n\nGiven $\\xi$, sample a bond configuration $\\omega\\in\\{0,1\\}^{E_n}$ with law $$\\label{eq:main-fk-law}\n \\phi_{n,\\xi}(\\omega)\n =\\frac1{Z_{n,\\xi}}\\,\n  2^{k_{\\mathrm w}(\\omega)}\n  \\prod_{e\\in E_n}p_e^{\\omega_e}(1-p_e)^{1-\\omega_e},\n \\qquad p_e=1-e^{-2\\beta_c(\\varepsilon)J_e}.$$ The boundary arc from $a_n$ to $b_n$ is wired and its complementary arc is free. Thus $k_{\\mathrm w}(\\omega)$ counts connected components after the vertices of the wired arc have been identified. The medial exploration separates the primal open cluster attached to the wired arc from the dual open cluster adjacent to the free arc. Write $\\gamma_n$ for this exploration in physical coordinates; it is $\\delta_n$ times the unit-grid exploration.\n\nCurves are oriented from their first endpoint to their second. We use the uniform curve metric modulo increasing reparametrization, with constant pauses immaterial. For representatives $\\gamma,\\widetilde\\gamma:[0,1]\\to\\mathbb C$, the bounded version of this metric is $$d_{\\mathrm{curv}}(\\gamma,\\widetilde\\gamma)\n =1\\wedge\\inf_{\\alpha,\\widetilde\\alpha}\n       \\sup_{t\\in[0,1]}\n       |\\gamma(\\alpha(t))-\\widetilde\\gamma(\\widetilde\\alpha(t))|,$$ where the infimum is over increasing homeomorphisms of $[0,1]$, followed by identification of curves at distance zero. Let $d_{\\mathrm{BL}}$ denote the bounded-Lipschitz metric on probability laws on this separable curve space. The conditional law $\\mu_{n,\\xi}$ of $\\gamma_n$ is a random probability measure, even though the environment is fixed when an interface is sampled.\n\n\n<div id=\"thm:main\" class=\"theorem\">\n\n**Theorem 1**. *There exists $\\varepsilon_0>0$ such that for every fixed $0<\\varepsilon<\\varepsilon_0$, every bounded marked Jordan domain $(D;a,b)$, and every deterministic filled square-lattice approximation satisfying <a href=\"#eq:jordan-approximation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:jordan-approximation\">[eq:jordan-approximation]</a>, $$d_{\\mathrm{BL}}\\bigl(\\mu_{n,\\xi},\n       \\operatorname{Law}(\\mathrm{SLE}_{16/3}(D;a,b))\\bigr)\n   \\xrightarrow[n\\to\\infty]{\\mathbb P_{\\mathrm{env}}}0.$$ The constant $\\varepsilon_0$ is independent of the domain and its approximation.*\n\n</div>\n\nThe disorder strength in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a> does not decrease with the mesh. The conclusion concerns the whole oriented curve, including its terminal portion near $b$.\n\n\n#### Background and the source of the difficulty\n\nThe random-cluster representation of Fortuin and Kasteleyn [[FK](#ref-FK)] and the coupling of Edwards and Sokal [[ES](#ref-ES)] connect Ising spin correlations to random connectivity. At the planar critical point, the fermionic observables of Smirnov [[Smirnov](#ref-Smirnov)] and Chelkak–Smirnov [[CS](#ref-CS)] give access to conformally invariant boundary-value problems. Chelkak, Duminil-Copin, Hongler, Kemppainen, and Smirnov [[CDHKS](#ref-CDHKS)] proved convergence of critical Ising and FK–Ising interfaces to their respective SLE limits. Crossing estimates stable under boundary conditions, as developed by Duminil-Copin, Hongler, and Nolin [[DCHN](#ref-DCHN)] and by Chelkak, Duminil-Copin, and Hongler [[CDH](#ref-CDH)], and the curve-compactness framework of Kemppainen–Smirnov [[KS](#ref-KS)] are central tools in passing from observables to interfaces.\n\nRandom perturbations of the interaction test the stability of this critical geometry. Harris’s criterion [[Harris](#ref-Harris)] identifies the two-dimensional Ising case as marginal, so the stability question is subtler than in a strictly irrelevant regime. Perturbative work of Dotsenko–Dotsenko, Shalaev, and Ludwig investigated weak random bonds and the resulting logarithmic corrections [[DD](#ref-DD), [Shalaev](#ref-Shalaev), [Ludwig](#ref-Ludwig), [LudwigCorrelations](#ref-LudwigCorrelations)]. In a recent rigorous treatment, Avérous–Mahfouf [[AM](#ref-AM)] obtain near-critical random-bond FK crossing estimates with disorder decreasing with the observation scale for $q>1$. Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a> concerns a fixed positive disorder strength and identifies the full interface law. The present result concerns the specific symmetric two-valued ferromagnetic disorder in <a href=\"#eq:random-couplings\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:random-couplings\">[eq:random-couplings]</a>. Its renormalization input is the companion manuscript [[Q](#ref-Q)], which identifies the critical point and constructs a local scale transformation whose bulk perturbations tend to zero. We state precisely the portions of that result used here in Section <a href=\"#sec:inputs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:inputs\">2</a>.\n\nVanishing bulk perturbations alone do not identify FK interface laws. An explored interface creates a random slit boundary, and conditional crossing probabilities must be compared in the remaining domain. Moreover, the available quenched bounds concern fixed physical scales; their environmental convergence has no rate that would justify a union bound over all microscopic domains or all possible slit histories. The proof must preserve these quantifiers when adapting the pure-model arguments.\n\n\n#### The proof and its reusable ingredients\n\nOur first step is a partition-function comparison on a fixed polygon. Near its boundary, bulk symmetry no longer supplies the cancellations used by the scale transformation. We replace those cancellations with pure Ising transfer estimates. Along a straight side the relevant projective distance decays quadratically in the ratio of scales. The quadratic decay makes the contribution of the boundary cells summable; at the finitely many corners and changes of boundary condition, a positive decay exponent suffices. Section <a href=\"#sec:boundary\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:boundary\">3</a> proves the transfer estimates, and Section <a href=\"#sec:polygon\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:polygon\">4</a> uses them to extend the scale transformation to fixed polygon boundaries.\n\nWe also need this comparison on polygons lying in a flat covering surface of a punctured planar domain. Such a polygon has ordinary square-grid charts, although its projection into the plane need not be one-to-one. Every lifted edge carries the disorder of its projected edge. The proof uses independence only within one injective chart and therefore allows different sheets to carry identical disorder. The pure four-point observable remains valid on these topological disks, as explained in Section <a href=\"#sec:pure\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:pure\">5</a>.\n\nBefore making these tests depend on an exploration, Section <a href=\"#sec:tightness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:tightness\">6</a> fixes the environment and controls its curves. From every mesh subsequence we extract a deterministic further subsequence on which all polygons in one countable stock have their pure limits and all annular barriers in a countable dense family hold almost surely. After fixing such an environment, those barriers give arm bounds and tightness of the exploration histories.\n\nThe second main step converts fixed tests into conditional tests after exploration. We puncture near finitely many boundary accesses and lift the remaining domain to a universal cover. Carefully chosen crosscuts control its conformal coordinate even near the slit boundary. Small deletions then confine the central portion of every possible crossing to a compact part of the cover, where a fixed polygon can test it. Section <a href=\"#sec:slits\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:slits\">7</a> gives the construction and the shared-edge comparison. This provides a way to transfer local partition-function information to adaptive domains while retaining the original lattice and its disorder.\n\nIf a conditional test failed, tightness would yield a convergent sequence of failing histories. The covering construction would produce one fixed polygon from the already convergent stock, contradicting its known limit. Thus the geometry may be chosen after the histories while the final test remains fixed before the mesh limit. No uniform rate over polygons or histories is required.\n\nTo recover the ordered curve from its driver, Section <a href=\"#sec:tightness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:tightness\">6</a> also proves a uniform approximation of the tip from positive conformal height. This uses a stopping-time version of the small-gate argument in [[KS](#ref-KS)]. Finally, Section <a href=\"#sec:identification\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:identification\">8</a> extracts bounded martingale tests from four-boundary-change probabilities. Two tests identify the Loewner driver as $\\sqrt{16/3}$ times Brownian motion. The tip estimate and a no-return bound at the target give convergence in the curve metric of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>.\n\nWe adapt the finite-graph drawings, closure comparisons, and martingale identification strategy of the companion manuscript [[C](#ref-C)], with their hypotheses stated at the point of use. The boundary extension and the covering construction are proved here; the homogeneous probabilistic inputs are replaced by the quenched estimates established in this paper.\n\n\n### Random-cluster conventions and imported estimates\n\nWe first identify the random couplings at the physical critical point. We then state the two inputs from the companion paper [[Q](#ref-Q)] that will be used throughout the proof: a local bound on FK arms and a scale transformation whose bulk perturbations vanish. The transformation acts on spin partition functions. Its extension to the boundary of a polygon is proved below; that extension is not part of the imported bulk estimate.\n\n\n#### The two probability spaces and planar duality\n\n\nFor a fixed bond environment $J$, a finite graph $G=(V,E)$, and a partition $\\pi$ of its boundary vertices, write $$\\phi_{G,J}^{\\pi}(\\omega)\n =\\frac{1}{Z_{G,J}^{\\pi}}\\,\n 2^{k_\\pi(\\omega)}\\prod_{e\\in E}v_e^{\\omega_e},\n \\qquad v_e=e^{2K_e}-1,\\quad K_e=\\beta J_e.\n \\label{eq:fk-odds}$$ Here $\\omega\\in\\{0,1\\}^E$, and $k_\\pi$ counts open components after the vertices in each part of $\\pi$ have been identified. A connection event in this paper always uses actual open edges, unless a specified boundary arc is explicitly allowed as part of the path. In particular, identifying two sets of vertices does not make an ordinary radial annulus crossing occur automatically.\n\n\nAt fixed $J$, the law <a href=\"#eq:fk-odds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fk-odds\">[eq:fk-odds]</a> has the spatial Markov property, satisfies FKG, and is increasing in the odds and in the boundary partition [[FK](#ref-FK), [FKG](#ref-FKG)]. These statements remain valid for inhomogeneous positive odds. Planar FK duality gives $$v_{e^*}^{\\,*}=\\frac{2}{v_e}.\n \\label{eq:dual-odds}$$ When completing a subgraph to a larger graph for an upper bound, we retain the actual odds on every shared edge and compare the induced boundary partitions.\n\nThe Edwards–Sokal coupling [[ES](#ref-ES)] relates this law to the Ising weight $\\exp(\\sum_e K_e\\sigma_x\\sigma_y)$. Given the spins, edges joining equal spins are independently open with probabilities $1-e^{-2K_e}$, and edges joining opposite spins are closed.
